# CLAUDE.md — ibms-app

This is the first IBMS engineering repo. **Standards, mandatory rules, domain
knowledge, and architecture decisions live in `ibms-brain` (imported above via git
submodule at `ibms-brain/`), not here** — `ibms-brain/meta/lex/` (mandatory),
`ibms-brain/meta/context/` (domain knowledge), `ibms-brain/meta/designs/` (why decisions
were made). Read the relevant `meta/context/` file before touching an area, and the
`meta/lex/` rules before any non-trivial change — most of them (money-decimal-jod,
workflow-state-transitions, maker-checker-segregation, sensitive-data-handling,
pdpl-sla-timers) apply the moment real domain code lands here, which it has not yet.

## What's New

| Date | Change | Action required |
|------|--------|-----------------|
| 2026-10-29 | **EVERY SCREEN IN THE ROUTER IS OFF `pageStyle` — AND FIVE ENGLISH COLUMN HEADERS WERE SITTING ON THE RECEIVABLES AGEING REPORT WHERE NO GATE COULD SEE THEM.** **(1) THE FINDING.** `AR_AGEING_BUCKET_LABEL` is a hardcoded ENGLISH-ONLY map in `apps/web/lib/`, rendered as the column headers of `/client-accounting` — *Current*, *1–30 days*, *31–60 days*, *61–90 days*, *90+ days* — on an Arabic-first product, on the screen that says what clients owe the office. **`hardcoded-ui-english.py` reports 0 undeclared and is correct by its own rules**: it scans `app/(app)` and `components/`, and this lives in `lib/`. That is the blind-spot class the script already records against itself — *it had never scanned `components/` at all* — ONE DIRECTORY FURTHER OUT. **MEASURED BEFORE BEING DESCRIBED**: exactly TWO label maps in `lib/` hold Latin prose, and the other (`LEAD_STATUS_LABEL`) has **NO CONSUMER AT ALL** — dead English rather than a live defect, because `LEAD_STATUS_LABEL_KEY` beside it is the bilingual one in use. **(2) FOUR OF THE FIVE CONVERT AND THE FIFTH DOES NOT.** Three are POINTERS at strings already rendering the identical range on `/dashboards/claims`; `current` is a SLICE of `commonCurrentLabel` with its inline colon dropped, substring and NFC asserted. **`d1_30` is left in English and recorded**: the nearest shipping string covers DAY ZERO, and on a receivables report day zero is the `current` bucket — a separate column one place to the left — so pointing at it would put a header over this column claiming a range it does not hold. A false label is worse than an English one. The row now reads four Arabic words and one English, which looks unfinished because it IS. **(3) THE DASHBOARDS GOT ORDERING AND GROUPING, WHICH IS §7's OWN LIMIT.** Every figure is the same figure from the same field with the same formatting; `metricScope` is untouched and decision 58 is not re-litigated — `/dashboards/policy`'s three figures each keep their own `FigurePeriod`, passed byte-for-byte into `Tile`'s `meta` slot, which is exactly where a per-figure period clause belongs. The claims ageing became a `BarList`, because four numbers side by side do not say which is the big one and *which bucket holds the money* is the whole question on that desk. **NO `Ring` for open-versus-closed, and that is a MEASUREMENT**: it is a proportion and the component exists for one, but `Ring` REQUIRES a caption carrying the raw count and no such string ships — the nearest carries the word ACTIVE, a different claim about a claim. **(4) THE LAST SEVEN KEEP THEIR HEADINGS, DELIBERATELY** — a customer's legal name, a policy line, the signed-in person's own name are not page TITLES, and making one into a title would have the frame claim the record is the page. **AND `/leads/[id]` AND `/policies/[id]` WRAP EVERY BRANCH IN A `<div>` RATHER THAN A `<main>`** — four each, one per state. A page with no `main` landmark is a page a screen reader cannot skip to; recorded, not fixed, because changing a page's landmark structure is more than a wrapper swap. **111 screens, 0 on `pageStyle`, 101 composing from `components/ui`; 1,455 → 1,132 inline sites across the batch.** **Verification, every gate re-run AFTER the last edit**: web typecheck 0 errors; web lint **8 warnings, 0 errors** (the baseline); web unit **23 files / 196 tests** from `apps/web` with i18n parity over the new derived key pair; every gate through `npm run check:screen-pass` exit 0 with every other budget UNMOVED; **Playwright 147 tests across 13 specs** green after a FORCED rebuild, and **the FULL a11y suite 85/85**; the table checker at its 3 declared pre-existing rows. | **`hardcoded-ui-english.py` DOES NOT SCAN `apps/web/lib/`** — two label maps there held Latin prose and one was live on screen. Before trusting a 0 from that gate on a screen rendering a label map, check where the map LIVES. **AND THE AGEING HEADERS ARE KEYS NOW** — `AR_AGEING_BUCKET_LABEL_KEY` with a deliberate `null` for `1–30 days` and a one-entry fallback beside it; do NOT point that `null` at `dclmAgeing0To30`, which covers a day this bucket does not. **A DASHBOARD FIGURE'S `FigurePeriod` GOES IN `Tile`'s `meta`** and must keep its own `scope` from `metricScope` — decision 58, which this batch may not re-open. |
| 2026-10-29 | **19 MORE SCREENS, AND A NOTICE THAT IS NOT AN ERROR IS WEARING THE ERROR TOKEN ON FIFTEEN OF THEM.** **(1) THE FINDING, surfaced by the converter removing an import and the compiler naming every orphaned use.** `style={errorStyle}` is used on fifteen screens for something that is NOT a failure — a REDUCED-CAPABILITY notice (*you can see this list, you cannot approve from it*) and permission refusals. **THEY WERE NOT CONVERTED TO `ErrorState`**: that would put a red danger box around a sentence telling somebody the system is working as intended for their role, which is the opposite of what the four-states work is for. The import is restored and the class is recorded — a reader cannot tell a refusal they can do nothing about from a failure they should report, `Note tone="info"` is the shape this product already has for it, and choosing per site is a per-screen judgement rather than a sweep. **(2) TEN OF THESE WERE THE PREVIOUS BATCH'S OWN SCREENS.** `design-pass-sections.py` converts sections and tables and deliberately leaves the page wrapper alone, so they kept `<main style={pageStyle}>` and their heading; running the list converter over them afterwards is what finished them. **(3) AND DOING THAT EXPOSED THREE WAYS THE TWO TOOLS GET IN EACH OTHER'S WAY, each found by `tsc`.** The row-pairing scan counted every `<Tr` opener including ones already closed, so it hunted `</tr>` tags that no longer existed and refused a file with *2 row(s) opened, 0 closed* when nothing was wrong with it — it counts the UNCLOSED ones now. Both tools added their own `components/ui` block, and every shared name became `TS2300: Duplicate identifier` — the imports tool MERGES first, so it is idempotent. And the import-restore anchored on `^import .*$`, which matches the first LINE of a multi-line block, so the new statement was spliced INSIDE an open `import {` on seven screens. **(4) `/opportunities` HAS NO PLAYWRIGHT SPEC** — the second screen found with none after `/renewal-cases`, again by running the filters through `node scripts/playwright-specs.mjs`. **160 → 147 on these 19 files; `SCREEN_BUDGET` 1184 → 1171.** **Verification, every gate re-run AFTER the last edit**: web typecheck 0 errors; web lint **8 warnings, 0 errors** (the baseline); every gate through `npm run check:screen-pass` exit 0 with every other budget UNMOVED; **Playwright 237 tests across 18 specs** green after a FORCED rebuild, including every @a11y; the table checker at its 3 declared pre-existing rows. | **DO NOT CONVERT A `style={errorStyle}` PARAGRAPH TO `ErrorState` WITHOUT READING WHAT IT SAYS** — fifteen of them are reduced-capability notices or permission refusals, where a danger box is a false claim about what went wrong. **RUN `design-pass-convert.py` BEFORE `design-pass-sections.py`, AND `design-pass-imports.py` LAST** — the imports tool is idempotent and merges, the other two are not and will each add their own import block. **AND AN `^import .*$` ANCHOR MATCHES THE FIRST LINE OF A MULTI-LINE BLOCK**: inserting a statement there splices it inside an open `import {`. |
| 2026-10-29 | **THE CROWDED SCREENS — AND THE a11y SUITE FOUND A KEYBOARD TRAP EVERY TABLE IN THIS PRODUCT ALREADY HAD.** **(1) 52 AXE VIOLATIONS ON ONE SCREEN, the moment `/retention-disposal`'s six hand-rolled tables became six `Table`s. `scrollable-region-focusable`: the component wraps its table in an `overflow-x: auto` div, and **a scroll container nothing can focus is a region a keyboard user cannot scroll AT ALL** — everything past the fold is simply unreachable. **THE HAND-ROLLED TABLES HAD THE SAME DEFECT AND THE SUITE NEVER SAID SO**: each screen wrote its own overflow div, and axe raises this only when the region actually OVERFLOWS, so whether it was reported depended on the fixture's data being wide enough. Moving every table onto one component is what made one fix reach all of them AND what made the defect loud enough to see. `tabIndex={0}` is the whole fix and the focus ring goes with it; no `role="region"`, because a region needs an accessible NAME to be worth announcing and that is a new string per table in both languages on 40-odd screens. **(2) THE CROWDED SCREEN, AND WHAT MADE IT CROWDED.** `/retention-disposal` is SIX `<section>`s on one page, each a bare `<h2>` over a table that brought its own borders and its own minimum width — 56rem here, 64rem there, `width: 100`-percent somewhere else — so a reader scrolling met three different horizontal behaviours and nothing said where one block of work ended. A section is a CARD WITH A TITLE now. `design-pass-sections.py` finds BOTH closing tags by PAIRING with a depth count rather than a blanket replace, because `</section>` and `</tr>` nest and a screen where one converts and another does not would get a `</Card>` closing a `<section>`. **259 → 204 on 11 files; `SCREEN_BUDGET` 1239 → 1184.** **(3) THE `data-*` TRAP TWICE MORE — SO IT IS A GUARD NOW.** `Tr` still named only `data-testid`, so `/retention-disposal`'s `data-notice-for` and `/screening-health`'s `data-capability` went nowhere; `Card` named none, so two section hooks did too. **Five components in three batches, every one found by a failing test rather than by the compiler.** `apps/web/test/data-attributes.test.ts` requires every `components/ui` component to forward, with declared exemptions that fail in BOTH directions. **(4) AND THE GUARD HAD TWO HOLES OF ITS OWN, BOTH FOUND BY PLANTING IT.** It checked per FILE, so planting the forwarding away from `Card` left `Card.tsx` still containing `PageHeader`'s spread and the guard passed on a component that had stopped forwarding. Then it checked for the BARE NAME, which matches the DESTRUCTURE — so a planted `Card` that still collected the attributes and no longer spread them passed again. It matches `{...dataAttributes}`, the spread onto an element, and the plant now dies naming `Card.tsx#Card`. **A guard whose own plant survives is not a guard**, which is this repository's own standard applied to the thing doing the checking. **(5) AND IT NAMED TWO THINGS THAT WERE FINDINGS ABOUT THE MATCHER** rather than the code: two exported predicates that return booleans and render nothing, and `Button`, whose props are a NAMED interface declared above the function so a per-component slice cannot see its `HTMLAttributes` extension — fixed by requiring the body to render markup, and by allowing that one route at file level with the limit written down. **Verification, every gate re-run AFTER the last edit**: web typecheck 0 errors; web lint **8 warnings, 0 errors** (the baseline); web unit **23 files / 196 tests** from `apps/web`, +1 file for the new guard; every gate through `npm run check:screen-pass` exit 0 with every other budget UNMOVED; **Playwright 167 tests across 11 specs** green after a FORCED rebuild, and **the FULL a11y suite 85/85** — which is the one that matters here, because the `Table` change touches every table in the product. | **`Table`'s SCROLL CONTAINER IS FOCUSABLE AND MUST STAY SO** — removing `tabIndex={0}` makes every wide table in the product unreachable past the fold for a keyboard user, and axe only catches it on a screen whose fixture data is wide enough to overflow. **EVERY COMPONENT IN `components/ui` MUST FORWARD `data-*`** — `apps/web/test/data-attributes.test.ts` fails by name otherwise, and an exemption needs a real reason and comes OFF the list the moment that component starts forwarding. Use `DataAttributes` from `components/ui/data-attributes.ts`; do not name `data-testid`, which has now been one name short five times. **AND WHEN YOU WRITE A GUARD, PLANT IT AGAINST THE THING IT CHECKS** — this one passed two plants before it worked, once because it read the file instead of the component and once because it read the destructure instead of the spread. |
| 2026-10-29 | **THE DESIGN BATCH, 40 MORE SCREENS — AND THE `data-*` TRAP FOR THE THIRD TIME, WHICH THIS TIME COST A TEST.** **(1) NAMING ONE ATTRIBUTE DOES NOT CLOSE THAT CLASS.** `ErrorState` named `data-testid` explicitly, which was the pilot's own fix after three hooks were silently dropped. Converting `/insurer-directory`'s error paragraph carried its `data-line-error` across faithfully, **into a component that dropped it** — `tsc` clean, the screen rendering perfectly, and the only thing that said so was a Playwright assertion looking for the attribute. A screen hangs its OWN hook on a refusal, a pill, a cell, so a list of names is always one short. `Badge`, `Table`, `Td`, `Th`, `EmptyState` and `ErrorState` now all take a template-literal `data-` index signature and spread it, which makes the forwarding a property of the SIGNATURE rather than something a reader must verify in the body. **(2) THE ERROR TRANSFORM WAS REPLACED RATHER THAN WIDENED A FIFTH TIME.** It had grown a variant per shape — bare, inline, carrying `data-testid`, carrying some other hook, wearing a `marginTop` override — and every new screen found one more. **A list of shapes that grows by one per file is a matcher that has not understood what it is matching.** One rule now matches the PARAGRAPH and rides any surviving attributes across; the `marginTop` is DROPPED on purpose, because `pageStackStyle`'s own gap is what spaces a screen's children now. **(3) THE GROUP WAS DRIVEN WITH AUTOMATIC REVERT-ON-FAILURE.** The converter refuses a file whose shape it does not recognise; this covers the other half — a file it DOES recognise and gets wrong — by letting the compiler arbitrate: convert a group, typecheck once, revert exactly the screens `tsc` names, repeat until quiet. **22 screens were reverted that way and are untouched**, listed in the register as still owed rather than shipped half-converted. **(4) FIVE MORE SHAPES the converter had not seen**, each found by a refusal or by `tsc`: a heading with `opacity: 0.8` and no `maxWidth`; an `<h1>` with no description; an import carrying `pageStyle` alongside other names; the MULTI-LINE import block prettier makes once that list is long enough — where the first fix spliced the new statement INSIDE the open block and gave three screens `TS1003` — and a cell whose style sits on its own line because it carries two attributes. **(5) AND MY SHELL'S CWD DRIFTED INTO `apps/web`**, so `git status --short` printed paths relative to there and a measurement script read every file as having ZERO inline styles before the change. Caught because *before: 0* is not a number a real tree produces. **370 → 319 on these 40 files; `SCREEN_BUDGET` 1290 → 1239.** **Verification, every gate re-run AFTER the last edit**: web typecheck 0 errors; web lint **8 warnings, 0 errors** — the baseline, with five leftover multi-line-import names cleared; web unit **22 files / 192 tests** from `apps/web`; every gate through `npm run check:screen-pass` exit 0 with rendered-identifiers 12, typed-identifier 12 / 0 required, four-states 1+9+2 and both route budgets all UNMOVED; `check:labels`, `check:css-properties`, `check:loaders`, `check:absence` clean; **Playwright 312 tests across 29 specs green including every @a11y**, after a FORCED rebuild; the table checker at its 3 declared pre-existing rows. | **`data-*` IS FORWARDED BY SIX PRIMITIVES NOW** — `Badge`, `Table`, `Td`, `Th`, `EmptyState`, `ErrorState`. When you write a seventh that a screen might hang a hook on, give it the same index signature rather than naming `data-testid`: that is the fix that was tried twice and was one name short both times. **AND 22 SCREENS WERE REVERTED BY THE COMPILER AND ARE STILL OWED** — the register names them; the heaviest (`/retention-disposal` at 70 sites, `/screening-health` at 37, `/regulatory-compliance` at 25, `/sla-dashboard` at 22) are the crowded screens §4(c) is about and want hand work, not a wider matcher. |
| 2026-10-29 | **THE DESIGN BATCH, 33 SCREENS — AND A FALLING RATCHET THAT MEANT THE INSTRUMENT HAD GONE BLIND.** **(1) THE FINDING THAT MATTERS MOST IS ABOUT THE GATES, NOT THE SCREENS.** `check:typed-identifier-inputs` went **12 → 11** and asked to be tightened, which reads exactly like a field being closed. It was not: that gate matches `<input` on the line carrying `value={…Id}`, and converting `/complaints` onto `Field` + `TextInput` — which is the whole point of the pass — removed the `<input`, so a field that still asks a person to type a claim id by hand became INVISIBLE. **A falling budget that means the matcher stopped looking is the worst shape a ratchet has**, because it is indistinguishable from the work being done and it is silent; this repository has recorded the class twice (`unreachable-routes.py`'s ambiguous drop, `rendered-identifiers.py` reading `??` as a ternary) and this is the first time the design work itself caused it. **`four-states.py` HAD THE SAME EXPOSURE ONE STEP AHEAD** — its rule 1 compares the first FORM against the first `<table`, so a converted screen would read as having no table and the rule would stop being checkable on exactly the screens the pass has reached. **Both widened to the PRIMITIVE AS WELL AS the raw element**, never instead of it, and the count is back at 12 because nothing closed. **(2) 33 SCREENS, 273 → 145 INLINE SITES, `SCREEN_BUDGET` 1418 → 1290.** They share one shape and were converted by an EXACT-MATCH converter that REFUSES a file where a transform it was told to apply does not match — seven screens refused and are listed in the register as still owed, rather than half-converted. **Every transform is a real defect rather than a rename**: `opacity` as muting is what `globals.css` itself bans and is the cause of this product's recorded contrast regression; a hand-rolled table carries its own `minWidth` so no two screens agreed when one starts scrolling, and TWO had no scroll container at all; `0.2rem`/`0.3rem`/`0.4rem`/`1.75rem` are off the five-step scale. **(3) FIVE DEFECTS IN MY OWN CONVERTER, EVERY ONE CAUGHT BY `tsc`.** A non-greedy DOTALL body matcher ran past `const head`'s one-liner and deleted three more style constants (a brace-free body matcher cannot cross a declaration). A pattern built from the FILE's JS quote style missed `role="alert"`, because prettier writes JSX ATTRIBUTES with double quotes whatever the surrounding code uses — and the import removal is a separate transform that DID apply, so four screens were left referencing a deleted name. The two table shapes could both partially fire, leaving an opening `<div>` whose close had been eaten. `<td style={cell}>` with the bracket in the pattern skipped every ATTRIBUTED cell while its `</td>` still converted. And the row closer matched only where a `</Td>` sat immediately above, which is false for any row whose last cell is conditional — replaced by a real paired scan. **(4) AND A SWEEP OF MINE WAS DEFEATED BY MY OWN COMMENT** — the unread-constant remover counts occurrences, and a comment I had just written QUOTED the constant's name, so it counted two and spared the declaration. That is `check:css-properties`'s recorded limit (a check that reads TEXT cannot tell a comment from code) recurring inside my own tool; comments are stripped before counting here, which that gate deliberately does NOT do because it needs to see a reference inside a commented-out block. **(5) `Badge`, `Table`, `Td` AND `Th` NOW FORWARD `data-*` THROUGH A TEMPLATE-LITERAL INDEX SIGNATURE.** TypeScript exempts hyphen-containing JSX attributes from excess-property checking, so `data-claim-status` on any component type-checks and goes NOWHERE — the defect that silently killed three hooks in the pilot. Naming one attribute the way `Tr` names `data-testid` is not enough for a cell or a pill, because every screen hangs its own; the index signature accepts the family and makes the forwarding a property of the SIGNATURE. **(6) 35 TEST LOCATORS WERE PINNED TO THE ELEMENT A REFUSAL HAPPENS TO SIT IN** (`p[role="alert"]`), and `ErrorState` is a `div` that IS `role="alert"`. Re-pointed to the ROLE, which is the accessibility property the assertion is about — NARROWER where it matters and wider only about the tag. **That immediately surfaced a latent collision**: Next renders its own route announcer with `role="alert"`, so all 34 page-wide locators resolved to TWO elements under strict mode. It had always been there and was masked because the announcer is a `<p>` too. Scoped to `main`, which is also the stronger claim — a refusal announced outside the screen's own landmark is a refusal in the wrong place. **(7) THREE SCREENS GOT MORE THAN THE MECHANICAL PASS.** `/claims` rendered its status pill and its two TRIAGE signals in one identical grey, so a queue built to answer *what needs me next* looked flat; the two actionable ones take `warning` and keep their words. `/complaints` lost four `aria-label`s, TWO of them wrong — `complaintsCategoryAria` said «التصنيف» where the visible label says «التصنيف (اختياري)», so a screen-reader user was not told the field is optional — and a real defect: `escalations.length \|\| "—"` renders an em dash for ZERO, the `0 \|\| x` shortcut this product has now paid for three times. `/claims-analytics`' totals row carried `{ ...cellStyle, fontWeight: 600 }` once per column, so `Tr` gained a `total` variant. **(8) AND `/renewal-cases` HAS NO PLAYWRIGHT SPEC AT ALL** — found by running the filters through `node scripts/playwright-specs.mjs`, which refuses a filter matching nothing, where `npx playwright test` would have run a smaller suite and still passed. Recorded, not written: a spec is not a design change. **Verification, every gate re-run AFTER the last edit**: web typecheck 0 errors; web lint **8 warnings, 0 errors** — down from a 10 baseline, two pre-existing ones cleared (the pilot's own `AppNavbar` organization-name derivation, dead since the rail took it over, and two unused `AppNav` imports); web unit **22 files / 192 tests** from `apps/web`; every gate through `npm run check:screen-pass` exit 0 with rendered-identifiers 12, typed-identifier 12 and four-states at 1+9+2 all UNMOVED; `check:labels`, `check:css-properties`, `check:loaders` clean; **Playwright 171 tests across 32 specs green including every @a11y**, after a forced rebuild; the table checker at its 3 declared pre-existing rows. | **A FALLING RATCHET IS NOT AUTOMATICALLY PROGRESS — CHECK WHETHER THE MATCHER STILL SEES WHAT IT COUNTS.** `typed-identifier-inputs.py` and `four-states.py` now match the PRIMITIVE as well as the raw element; any other gate keying on `<input`, `<select>`, `<table` or `<p role="alert">` has the same exposure as the pass converts more screens. **`Badge`, `Table`, `Td`, `Th` AND `Tr` FORWARD `data-*`** — a screen's machine hook survives the move onto a primitive, which it did not before. **SCOPE EVERY `[role="alert"]` LOCATOR TO `main`**: Next's route announcer carries that role on every page, so a page-wide locator is a strict-mode violation waiting for the moment both are present. **RUN PLAYWRIGHT THROUGH `node scripts/playwright-specs.mjs`** — a filter matching no file is SILENTLY IGNORED and the smaller suite still passes; that is how `/renewal-cases` was found to have no spec. **AND `scripts/measurements/design-pass-convert.py` IS EXACT-MATCH AND REFUSES** — when it refuses a screen, convert that screen by hand; do not loosen a transform to make it land, because every one of its five defects was a loosened pattern crossing a boundary it should not have. |
| 2026-10-29 | **THE OWNER ANSWERED ALL THREE WORDS SHE WAS OWED, AND NONE OF THEM HAD TO BE WRITTEN — PLUS THE PUSH THAT A `push: true` REPORTED AS PERMITTED AND WAS NOT.** **(1) THE SIGN-IN PANEL'S PROSE WAS IN HER OWN BRAND PACK ALL ALONG.** The pilot shipped that panel deliberately wordless and recorded why — the i18n tree holds no product tagline to slice, and a sentence written here on the first screen a client reads is exactly what rule 8(d) prevents. She answered by pointing at the source that was missing: her Arabic and English flyers, which carry the headline, the product sentence and six capability lines, and reference page B, which composes three of them. **THE ARABIC BYTES CAME FROM THE REFERENCE PAGE'S HTML AND NOT FROM MY READING OF THE FLYER**, because the flyer is a PNG and transcribing Arabic from an IMAGE is precisely where the shadda/vowel trap bites, while the reference is text that copies exactly and that she has already looked at. **(2) THE EM DASH BECAME THREE WORDS, NOT ONE, AND THE INSTRUCTION WITH THEM IS HALF THE DECISION.** «لم يُقيَّم بعد» (not done yet), «لا ينطبق» (does not apply — SLICED from `providerOfficesLicenceNotApplicable`, not typed), «غير متوفّر» (held but unresolvable), with *a site that does not fall cleanly into one of the three keeps its em dash and is RECORDED*. Measured over the pilot's five sites: **three converted and two did not**, plus four more left alone — *not recorded* is not one of her three, a cheque has a drawee bank and a card an issuer so a blank there is a real gap, and *no action taken yet* is a different verb from *not ASSESSED yet*. **An unrecognised channel type falls to the dash rather than to «لا ينطبق»**, because `channelType` is a free `String` whose vocabulary lives in a schema comment and defaulting an unknown value to *does not apply* would be the system asserting what it cannot know. **(3) AND THE NFC GUARD CAUGHT MY OWN TYPING OF HER WORD, which is the first time that trap has been stopped before it reached a file**: «لم يُقيَّم بعد» came out `064A 0651 064E` — shadda before fatha — where NFC orders by combining class and the vowel (30) sorts before the shadda (33). The two render identically and compare unequal. Normalising is not editing her word, NFC being canonical equivalence, and the reordering is PRINTED by the script rather than happening silently. **(4) THE ARABIC EMAIL SUBJECTS CARRY THE PRODUCT'S NAME**, her reason being the sharp half — the three were never WRONG and never carried the codename; they described the system because there was no name to put there. It is a SUBSTITUTION rather than an addition, because the English siblings have read `WASEET 360` all along and keeping both would make the Arabic subject say more than the English. **One BODY line went with them as a stated judgement beyond her ruling**, its English sibling having always read *from WASEET 360*. Asserted over EVERY template kind rather than over the three that exist, because the failure guarded against is a FOURTH template shipping generic in Arabic — and the ABSENCE of the old descriptor is asserted separately, since a subject that gained the name while keeping the descriptor beside it would satisfy the first check and still say two things. Planted: both die, each for its own reason. **(5) THE PUSH, AND TWO DIAGNOSES WORTH KEEPING.** `git -c credential.helper=…` **APPENDS** to the existing list, so the first attempt was answered by the machine's own OAuth helper and the supplied credential was never consulted — clearing the list with an empty `-c credential.helper=` first is what made it the only source. Then `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}` reported **`push: true`**, which is the ACCOUNT's access to the repo and NOT the credential's grants, so it reads as permission the push does not have; what settled it is that **GitHub names `workflow` scope BY NAME when that is the only thing missing**, so a refusal that does not name it means write access is absent entirely. | 2026-10-12 | **CI WAS RIGHT TWICE, and the second-order mistake is the one to keep: I SUBSTITUTED `build` FOR `typecheck` AND WENT BLIND TO EVERY SPEC FILE.** `npm run build --workspace=api` compiles through `tsconfig.build.json`, which **EXCLUDES spec files**; `npm run typecheck` is the gate that includes them. After item (f) I found a single pre-existing TS2719 in `employee.config.spec.ts`, established it reproduces on an unmodified HEAD, and then used `build` as my compile check for the rest of the session — so CI found **six errors across five files** that my local gates could not see. `commission-agreement.service.spec.ts` passed a string at 16 call sites after item (h) widened `create` to take the full caller, in **TWO SPELLINGS of the same fixture** (`'mgr-1'` and `'actor-1'`), which is why my first pass fixed 11 and missed 5; `policy.service.spec.ts`, `rfq.service.spec.ts` and `duty-segregation-readiness.spec.ts` (twice) were short the `InsuranceLineRepository` argument; and `employee-cbj-approval.e2e-spec.ts` typed a role as `RoleName`, **the legacy enum from before office-scoped roles**, which cannot name `OFFICE_ADMINISTRATOR` — that spec PASSED at runtime because vitest compiles through swc, which strips types instead of checking them, the same trap `sample:release` hit earlier today. **The lesson is not "run typecheck" — it is that WORKING AROUND A BROKEN GATE IS HOW A GATE STOPS CATCHING THINGS.** **CORRECTION, and it is the sharpest lesson of the three: the TS2719 WAS MINE AND CI DOES REPORT IT.** I wrote here that "CI's own typecheck reports ZERO TS2719" — FALSE. I had read CI's error list through `head -14` and the TS2719 sat below the cut: the head-truncated-grep failure this repo has already recorded three times, this time used to justify a claim in writing. And my "it reproduces on unmodified HEAD" test was CONTAMINATED — I stashed the source but `node_modules/.prisma/client` still held item (g)'s regenerated columns, so the stashed spec compiled against a client from the future. **The actual cause was item (g):** `employee.config.spec.ts`'s fixture uses the FULL `Employee` model type precisely so a new column cannot be added without a decision being made there — its own header says so — and it fired exactly as designed while I called it an artefact. Adding `cbjApprovalStatus` and `cbjApprovalDate` takes `npm run typecheck` to **6/6, zero errors**. Two wrong diagnoses, both from an experiment I did not control. **AND I SHIPPED THE DRIFT CLASS ITEM (g) IS ABOUT, IN ITEM (g).** `person-and-login.spec.ts` mocks the employee detail read and did not carry `cbjApproval`, so the panel's `approval.status` threw during render — taking down the reveal, the training list, the correction form and the de-provisioning checklist with it. I updated `employees.spec.ts`'s fixture because I was editing that file, and never asked which OTHER specs mock the same endpoint. Measured properly: **four specs mock an employee detail read**; `employee-reveal.spec.ts` goes to `/employees/reveal` (a different page, which is why it passed) and `users.spec.ts` only asserts a link, so one fixture needed the field. **The panel now DEGRADES rather than taking the page down**, as a decision rather than a patch: it renders "the server did not send this" and NOT "no approval on record", because those are different facts and the second would be a wrong claim about a person's regulatory standing — the api e2e still guarantees the field is sent, this only bounds the damage, and a Playwright test asserts BOTH halves (the notice appears AND the rest of the page survives). **Verification**: api typecheck down to the one known error from five files of them; api lint and build exit 0; api unit **255 files / 3263 tests** exit 0; api e2e 2 files / 5 tests; web typecheck and lint exit 0; web unit **136 tests**; Playwright employees + person-and-login + employee-reveal + users **40/40**, and the FULL web suite **759/760** — the one failure is `permission-refusal.spec.ts:157`, which passes in isolation and is contention in a 760-test run, not a finding. | **`npm run build -w api` IS NOT A TYPECHECK** — `tsconfig.build.json` excludes specs, so a widened service signature or a changed constructor is invisible to it. Run `npx tsc -p apps/api/tsconfig.json --noEmit` — or `npm run typecheck` — and expect **ZERO errors**. There is no tolerated baseline: the one I thought was tolerated was my own defect (see the CORRECTION above). **Never conclude "CI does not report X" from a `head`-truncated error list**, and never test "does this pre-exist" by stashing source while leaving a regenerated Prisma client in place — the stashed code then compiles against a client from the future. **Never work around a gate that has a standing error — establish what the baseline is and read past it**, because substituting a weaker gate is how six errors reached CI. **A vitest-passing spec is not a type-checked spec**: swc strips types. **`ensureRole` takes a plain `string`, not `RoleName`** — that enum predates office-scoped roles and cannot name `OFFICE_ADMINISTRATOR`. **When an api view gains a field a screen reads, grep for EVERY spec that mocks that endpoint** — four do for the employee detail — and widen them all in the same change. **A component reading a hand-copied HTTP field should not take its whole page down**: render a distinct "the server did not send this" state, never a default that makes a false claim. |

Follows the `InsurerDirectory` precedent exactly, because the problem is the same: a reader who needs facts spanning every office, where every office's rows are otherwise unreachable by RLS. **`security_invoker = false` IS the mechanism** — the view runs with its OWNER's privileges, whom Postgres exempts from the underlying RLS policies, and it discards everything it must not disclose BEFORE returning anything. **WHAT IS IN IT AND THE DISCLOSURE THAT CREATES.** Office state only, 18 columns: identity, status, broker kind, the current licence's number / expiry / standing, how many licensed branches carry no business, user counts, and when the office was last used. **No customer, policy, premium or commission data, and NO personal data — a property of the column list rather than a filter**: `lastActivityAt` is a MAX over the office's sessions, so it carries no `userId`, no name and no email. "When did anybody in this office last use the system" is a fact about the office; "when did Ahmad last sign in" is a fact about Ahmad, and the view cannot express it. The user figures are COUNTS, and a count is not a person. **THE DISCLOSURE IS NAMED RATHER THAN DISCOVERED**: unlike the insurer directory, which discloses facts about third-party INSURERS, this discloses facts about other BROKERAGE OFFICES — who are each other's competitors — and `dormantLicensedBranches` says another office is licensed for a line and writes no business in it. Before adding a column, read the migration header: this is a CROSS-OFFICE read, and the column list is the privacy and commercial boundary. The brief's own words are *"refuse to grant BOTH TYPES otherwise"*, so the one-type shortfall is **REPORTED** on the view and the screen, never refused. `requiredBefore < 2` is the second half: once an office legitimately holds both types, adding a third branch inside a type it already has must not be refused because a later CBJ suspension dropped the count — that would lock the office out of correcting its own branch list, and the correction is not what caused the shortfall. **ONLY `CURRENT` COUNTS, AND ONLY SOMEBODY WHO HAS NOT LEFT** — `approvalCounts` is an explicit equality rather than `!== 'CANCELLED'` so a fourth status fails CLOSED, and `countCurrentApprovals` carries `terminationDate: null` because counting a resignation would let an office satisfy a staffing minimum with a vacancy. **THE REFUSAL LIVES ON THE DTO HERE, WHICH IS THE OPPOSITE OF ITEM (e) AND FOR THE SAME REASON**: item (e)'s rule was about a field's ABSENCE, which `@IsOptional()` skips; this rule is about a value that is PRESENT, which `@IsOptional()` never skips — so one decorator at two declaration sites covers all three write paths. **`employee-cbj-approval.guard.spec.ts` IS THE "NOT A SECOND ROLE SYSTEM" GUARD**, reading the six files of the authorization path itself and failing if any of them so much as mentions the marker, with an existence check on each so a rename cannot turn it into a loop over zero files. **ELEVEN PLANTS, EVERY ONE KILLING EXACTLY ITS OWN TEST** — and three of them taught me something. So a licence's types ARE the distinct categories of its branches; a type column beside the branch would be a second home for one fact, and the copy is the one that drifts. `typesInScope` returns them in CATALOGUE order rather than insertion order, so the answer cannot depend on which branch somebody happened to record first. **`BrokerLicenseScopeLine` follows the established two-nullable-FK shape** (the four MUST models all carry it) with a CHECK making "exactly one, never both, never neither" structural, and the composite tenant FK so office A's licence cannot list office B's bespoke line. **"THE SAME BRANCH TWICE" IS TWO PARTIAL UNIQUES, NOT A COMPOUND ONE** — a compound unique over a nullable column admits UNLIMITED rows whose column is NULL (§ 1.25, the trap that let six insurers hold two open commission agreements), and here that is the COMMON case since every standard-line row has a NULL office-line id and vice versa. `db:divergence` is blind to a partial index, so the migration asserts both by reading `pg_get_indexdef` and requiring the WHERE clause to still be there — an index recreated without it keeps its name and starts rejecting every second standard-line row instead. **ART. 3/a IS ENFORCED AT SERVICE GRAIN, BY NECESSITY.** Item (b) deferred the one-type rule here, and here it meets the database's limits: deciding it needs `Organization.brokerKind`, the scope rows AND the catalogue's `category` — three tables — and a Postgres CHECK compares columns of ONE row of ONE table. A trigger could, but would have to fire on this table and on `brokerKind` changing, putting one rule in two places. So it lives in `BrokerLicenseScopeService`, is recorded as service-grain rather than left for a reader to assume a database guarantee, and **is proven by an api e2e over REAL HTTP rather than only a unit test** — the service being the only holder is exactly why the proof has to be where it is called. **ART. 4/a WARNS AND NEVER REFUSES, WITH THREE STATES NOT TWO.** 5,000 JOD per type up to 6 branches, 7,500 up to 12, 10,000 above 12, with the band boundaries asserted on BOTH sides of each edge (6 vs 7, 12 vs 13) because an off-by-one in a band table is the defect no mid-range test can see. "Per type" is read as SUMMED and flagged AS A READING — 4 general + 4 life requires 5,000 + 5,000 = 10,000, NOT the 7,500 a single eight-branch type would need, which is the whole point of "per type"; a reading that can only make a warning appear is a safe place to be approximately right and say so. The third state is the one a careless screen would lose: **"no capital recorded" is a missing fact about the office, while "recorded and short" is a finding about its capital** — conflating them tells an office it is undercapitalised when nobody has typed the number. `Organization.paidUpCapital` is `Decimal(18,3)`, and **the money gate did its job unprompted**: it failed first, naming `Organization.paidUpCapital` as unclassified, until it was entered in `MONEY_DECIMAL_FIELDS`. **ONE ASSUMPTION IS NOW EXPLICIT INSTEAD OF IMPLICIT** — `broker-license-scope-assumptions.spec.ts`. An office cannot be licensed for a type in which it holds no branches, which the derivation makes UNREPRESENTABLE rather than merely disallowed. **The licence forms were NOT AVAILABLE and the file says so**: `WASEET360_Broker_Insurer_Data_Fields.xlsx` is in neither the repo nor Downloads (the only WASEET360 archive there is branding assets — logos, two flyers, a logo-concepts PDF), and the only repo text citing art. 4/a is Module 0's own. So it is an inference from the regulation's arithmetic, NOT a reading of a form, and the file refuses to let anyone upgrade it to one. The guard that earns its place matches the SHAPE of a type-storing field on either licence model, so a differently-named version of the same mistake is caught; planting a `category` column on the scope row kills it with its own explanation. **FOUR THINGS I GOT WRONG AND FIXED, each found by a check rather than by reading.** (1) `db:divergence` REFUSED the composite FK until it was named — correct, and the name I used was a third style where the repo already has nine `_same_org_fkey`; rolled back and renamed. (2) The migration directory said `licence` where every identifier says `license`, so a grep for `broker_license` migrations would have missed it; rolled back and renamed again. (3) **A DTO constraint that could never fire**: the obvious `@Validate` class-level check cannot work on these fields, because `@IsOptional()` installs a condition that skips EVERY validator on its property when the value is absent — so a constraint on either optional field would be skipped in precisely the case it exists for, a caller who sends NEITHER. The sibling that uses that pattern hangs it off a REQUIRED field. Moved into the service where it always runs, with both the neither and the both case proven over HTTP. (4) **A MISSING DI PROVIDER that TypeScript cannot see**: the module wiring omitted `OrganizationRepository`, and my own comment claimed the module already provided it — the only match was the comment itself. A missing provider is a RUNTIME failure, so the 200 on `GET /broker-license/scope` is what proves it. **AND TWO MORE FOUND BY PLANTING AND BY THE SCREEN TESTS.** Planting art. 3/a away killed the unit test and the e2e (`expected 422, got 201`) — and **leaked a row**: the LIFE branch was created by the very request whose `.expect(422)` threw, so it was never in the teardown's list, and the next run failed on an assertion about a branch nobody could see had been created. **A teardown that only reverses its own bookkeeping cannot clean up after a request that unexpectedly SUCCEEDED** — it now clears every branch on the term, and re-planting leaves 0 rows. And the scope section **did not render at all** on its first run: the api, the client and the markup were right, and I had never added the loaders to the mount effect. Nothing type-checks "is this loader actually called on mount"; the Playwright tests are the only layer that could catch it, and did. **Verification**: applied to dev AND db-test, `db:checksums`/`db:divergence` green on both; **from-empty check — 125 migrations from nothing, then the seed, with RLS FORCED, BOTH partial uniques and the tenant FK all present on a database built from scratch**; typecheck 6/6 exit 0; lint 3/3 exit 0; read it before adding one. **`ENUM_LABEL.InsuranceLineCategory` is the shared home for GENERAL/LIFE wording** — the labels live in `translations/insurers.ts`, which already owns line wording, not in a screen's own dictionary. **`Language` is `'AR'` / `'EN'`, not lowercase** — two comparisons were wrong and only `tsc` said so. **A class-level `@Validate` on an `@IsOptional()` field NEVER RUNS when the field is absent.** If the rule is about the ABSENCE of a field, it cannot live on that field — put it where it always executes and prove both halves over HTTP. **A missing module provider is invisible to TypeScript**: if a service gains a constructor dependency, the proof is an e2e that boots the app, not a typecheck. **An e2e teardown must clear the STATE, not replay its own list of writes** — a request that unexpectedly succeeds creates something the list never knew about. **Copy a working e2e helper; Inventing a warning the brief did not ask for is how a screen starts crying wolf. **ART. 11 — 45 CALENDAR DAYS, AND THE READING IS A DECISION.** Not business days: the deadline is a span before a fixed calendar date, and counting it in business days would move it EARLIER — making an office late for a filing the regulation calls timely. That is the same failure direction as the empty holiday calendar, where the error ran against the brokerage, so where a reading is ambiguous the literal one wins. `renewalApplicationDueBy` is plain millisecond arithmetic and a test crosses a LEAP-YEAR February on purpose (45 days before 2028-03-15 is 2028-01-30, where month arithmetic would give 01-31 and be wrong by a day in exactly the direction that reports an on-time filing as late). `renewalFiledOnTime` is true ON the deadline, not merely before it — asserted, because `<` instead of `<=` would make an office filing exactly on time late. **THE BY-OPERATION-OF-LAW EXTENSION.** After expiry an office that applied on time stays valid until the CBJ's decision issues; one that filed nothing, or filed late, expires. **"Until the decision issues" needs NO extra column**, and that is the design rather than a shortcut: the decision IS the next term being recorded, and a later term wins `pickCurrentTerm` the moment it exists — so the branch is only ever reached for the office's LATEST term, which by definition has no decision after it. A `renewalDecidedAt` column would be a second home for a fact already derivable from the series, free to drift from it. **A MANUAL LAPSE OUTRANKS THE EXTENSION, and the ORDERING IS ASSERTED**: a CBJ suspension is not cured by having applied for renewal, so the `status === 'lapsed'` check comes first; planting the inversion kills two named tests. **`validByOperationOfLaw` IS ITS OWN FLAG rather than folded into `isCurrentlyLapsed`**, because the two answer different questions and a screen needs both — "may this office place business" (it may) and "is this term past its printed expiry" (it is). Collapsed, an officer would see an expiry in the past beside a system that is content and nothing explaining why. **PLANTS, AND ONE OF THEM TAUGHT ME SOMETHING ABOUT PLANTING.** Dropping the renewal-pending rule kills exactly three tests — the pure rule, the view, and the actual placement gate — so the rule is observed at all three layers rather than only where it is written. Hand-write, apply, `migrate resolve`, then run `db:checksums`. **Open the draft PR on commit one** — `ci.yml` triggers on `push: branches: [main]` and `pull_request` only, so a feature-branch push runs NOTHING, and 24 commits once sat on one laptop with no copy anywhere. **Targeted locally, FULL in CI** (14.1 min there vs 75 min on a quiet laptop) — and keep CI for being the only environment that starts from NOTHING, not for being fast. **An exclusion assertion can pass because the row was never on the page** — planting a neutered filter showed `not.toContain(x)` going GREEN while the page filled with 200 unrelated rows and the fixtures fell off it. An entry that says "re-derive" survives being wrong; one that says "do these three things" gets followed. **`ensureRole` UPSERTS**, so a mistyped role name in an e2e is CREATED with no grants and every request 403s — cost a full run, and left a permissionless role in the cumulative test database. **api e2e specs share one database and cannot run concurrently**: a second Organization live in another run makes `signup` refuse, which surfaces as unrelated 500s. **db-test has crossed a HARD limit and a `testTimeout` bump can no longer reach it**: at 45,939 ACTIVE users (5.9M audit rows), `rbac`'s access-recertification `startCycle` now 500s on Prisma's 120s INTERACTIVE-TRANSACTION cap — inside the application, so the request dies at 120s however long the test waits. Not a product defect (no office has 45,939 users), and the same commit proves it: CI ran `rbac` **10/10 in 14.7 seconds** while this laptop failed 4/10 with single tests at 143–261 seconds, because CI builds its database from NOTHING. **Resetting the local e2e database is now STEP ZERO of starting a branch**, beside the draft PR (`verification-contract.md`) — minutes on a schedule instead of a day mid-feature, twice discovered as an emergency. `prisma migrate reset` refuses to run unattended for an AI agent and needs the user's explicit consent for that run; do NOT route around it with a manual `DROP DATABASE`. **Take any measurement that depends on the current contents FIRST** — a reset makes some gates answer ZERO for the wrong reason, which is how § 1.40 was found. **And the reset immediately found a real defect, which is the argument for doing it on a schedule**: `RoleRepository.findActiveUserIdsWithPermission` had NO `orderBy` while `pickReviewer` takes `[0]` of it and README called that "the first eligible member" — so which reviewer a subject got was decided by the query PLAN, on a segregation-of-duties control. It passed at 46,153 users and failed at 23. Now `orderBy: [{ grantedAt: 'asc' }, { userId: 'asc' }]`, a total order (§ 1.42). **The class: a consumer taking `[0]` imposes an ordering requirement nothing type-checks** — put the order, and the reason, in the query. **The insurer feature has SCREENS now** — `/insurers` list + register + detail, the first insurer UI in the app (74 nav links and not one of them was for insurers). Three properties are asserted rather than assumed: the list shows deactivated insurers BY DEFAULT (they keep their policies and are the row an administrator most needs to find — hiding them reads as deletion); the detail page keeps COMPANY facts and this office's RELATIONSHIP terms in SEPARATE headed sections, proven by asserting each fact is under its own heading and absent from the other; and the deactivation step shows all five impact counts with the two policy figures APART, proven by planting a sum and reading `Received: "5"`. Nav sits beside `/vendors` under Operations — both are counterparty registers, while "New business" is a pipeline of stages. **A nav entry must sit in a group every holder of its gating permission can see** — I put `/insurers` in Operations beside `/vendors` (both counterparty registers) and CI refuted it: `sidebar-executive.spec.ts` asserts an Executive sees NO Operations group, and an Executive holds `insurer.read`. The rule is not what KIND of thing a screen is but WHO holds its permission; both insurer entries moved to New business, where every holder already works. **Read the FLAKY count in a green CI run, not only the conclusion** (§ 1.47): the count went 2 → 2 → **4** across three consecutive runs, two of which concluded SUCCESS. All four are the CLAIMS block of `rfq.spec.ts`, and a cascade was ruled out (each builds its own context) — they share a SHAPE: ~16 sequential interactions before one assertion, against a 30s budget. A growing flaky count is a different problem from a stable one. **And `/insurer-directory`** — its own nav entry and its own permission, never a tab on `/insurers`, because `insurer.directory.read` is separate from `insurer.read` so an office can be given the market without its own panel. A refused line code renders as a REFUSAL with the API's sentence, NEVER as an empty page; proven by planting the swallow and watching the error vanish while the empty state appeared. Two empty states, because "no company matches your search" and "nobody writes this line" are different claims. **MEASURED (§ 1.44), because the insurer feature sat unreachable for weeks with every gate green: four API surfaces have NO web caller** — `admin/email-integration` (6 routes), `POST /imports/customers`, and the two known insurer gaps. The office administrator holds `email.integration.read`, `email.integration.manage` and `customer.bulk-import` with no way to exercise any of them. Recorded, not acted on. `auth/sso` and `orgs` are correctly absent (a provider callback and subdomain resolution are not nav destinations), and `customers/:customerId` was a false positive — a parameterised prefix cannot be grepped for literally. **§ 1.45: a cast that silences the compiler usually means the question was good** — `as never` on a concatenated translation key hid the fact that there were no structure labels in EITHER language. A total `satisfies Record<Union, string>` map is the form that cannot express it. **§ 1.46: the i18n guard caught a genuine REPEAT of what it was built for** — a dictionary created and merged but not registered, silently excluding 88 keys from the parity and collision checks, exactly as `auth.ts` once did. First time one of these guards has fired on its own stated scenario, which is the evidence the class earns its cost. **`translations.test.ts` is the guard that caught my omission**: it asserts every dictionary file on disk is REGISTERED and that each keeps exact AR/EN parity, so a new screen's dictionary cannot be half-wired. **A server-side refusal is only half a change — read the SCREEN that calls it.** Tightening the commission rate resolver would have turned `/commission`'s free-text line field into a 422 for anyone who typed, and two defects fell out of reading it: the resolver matched `code`/`nameEn` only, so on this ARABIC-PRIMARY platform the Arabic line name was a 422 (every test in that file was English — that is how it survived); and the screen's Arabic placeholder was worded differently from the seeded Arabic name, so following the hint had ALWAYS produced an unmatched rate. The field is now a `<select>` valued by CODE — language-independent submission, label follows the reader — with office additions filtered out because the API cannot resolve them, pinned by an option-count assertion. **The signup refusal named a route that could never help** — "provision users through POST /admin/users, which names the Organization explicitly", when `ProvisionUserDto` has no `organizationId` and the route only ever writes into the CALLER's office. Removed, not reworded: it now says a second office cannot currently be onboarded through the application, names Phase 4, and names the only two writers of `Organization` (both developer tools, neither reachable over HTTP). Pinned by a spec asserting the message does NOT mention `/admin/users`. § 1.11's 500 stays open by prior decision — and it caused its THIRD expensive diagnostic today. **A migration that converts rows and leaves the WRITERS is the shape to watch for** — it decays monotonically, and nothing fails while nothing reads the column. The four writers now populate the line FK and the money crossing (policy → governing commission rate) matches on it; proven by writing a row through each API path and reading the STORED row back, plus three plants. **§ 1.43: a baseline is a MEASUREMENT** — ask "does anything outstanding rest on this data?" in its yes/no form, and read what a reset breaks as a finding rather than noise. Both of today's findings came from there. **A generator's output is not evidence about the generator**: mine produced valid TypeScript that passed `tsc` and had silently dropped an export 86 spec files import — only reading the first `git diff` could catch that. **`npm run db:fixture:permissions` now exists** — the web e2e's permission grid said "GENERATED … regenerate rather than hand-edit" for months with no generator behind it; `verify.sh` gates the `--check` form. Read a generator's first diff: mine silently dropped an export 86 spec files import. **Read `IMPROVEMENTS.md`'s new opening principle** before the numbered entries: *prefer the form that cannot express the mistake over the form that requires you to notice it.* |

A NEW office gets that role and **no business roles at all**; nothing in the application creates an Organization (the only two writers are the seed and the demo script), so the rule lives in both writers plus a test asserting an Organization with no active route to user administration is a defect. **The reveal splits differ deliberately.** `employee.manage` → `employee.read`/`.create`/`.update` + `employee.national-id.reveal` (per ROUTE: its DTO accepts only that field); `customer.national-id.reveal` split out of `customer.360-view.read` **per FIELD**, because the same endpoint reveals a phone number a Sales Officer needs for their own customer — a route-level gate would have stopped an officer phoning their own client. Compliance alone holds both; the administrator and the Manager both lose the employee one, which is the point. KYC proven intact end to end. **THREE REAL DEFECTS THE WORK FOUND.** (1) **The AC1 measurement exposed grant drift in the demo office**: the first diff was empty on db-test but had ONE row on dev, because `seed.ts` grants permissions to the DEFAULT office's roles only — so every code added after a demo office was created never reached it, and Office B's administrator role was 3 codes behind the grid (25 across all eight of its roles). Fixed at the source with a mirror-from-the-default-office helper; with the drift closed the dev diff is empty too. (2) **`/settings/users` had no web e2e at all, and its grant dropdown was broken**: the options carried the role NAME while `grantRole` posts that value as `roleId`, which the API validates with `@IsUUID()` — every grant an administrator made from that dropdown was a 400, and the default (set from the first catalogue id) matched no option. Every new guard was proven by planting the regression and watching it fire: fourteen plants across the two reveal gates, the grid invariants, the orphaned office, the 23rd grant, the `isSystem` bypass, both UI reveal gates, the segregation warning, the step-up gate, the original dropdown bug restored, the fourth lockout route un-guarded, and an office's own role marked `isSystem`. | **A strict-subset grant set is what makes a "grant everyone this new role" migration provably safe.** Adding `insurer.relationship.manage` to the administrator would have broken that property, which is why it is deferred with the insurer backlog rather than for tidiness — `permissions.spec.ts` now asserts the subset so it cannot quietly stop holding. **The seed grants to the DEFAULT office only.** Any code added after an office was created never reaches that office's roles; It failed on the one CI step no local script covers, on a database fresh enough to need a CREATE rather than an upsert-existing. Still to come: Phase 2 (convert the 12 role-name-keyed decision points), Phase 3 (`status`/`isSystem`, Role CRUD + matrix, OFFICE_ADMINISTRATOR), Phase 4 (unified User/Employee screen), Phase 5 (migration rehearsal + the remaining tests). |
| 2026-09-17 | **The backup restore drill had failed every scheduled run since 2026-08-31 — three stacked faults, each hidden behind the one before it, and the third was the drill lying about what it verified.** **Fault 1 — the seed crashed.** Nothing in this job ever ran `prisma generate`: `npm ci` has no postinstall hook, and unlike `ci.yml` this workflow runs no turbo task (turbo's graph builds `@ibms/db`, which is where `ci.yml` quietly gets its client). `RoleName` was `undefined` and the seed died on `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'SALES_RELATIONSHIP_OFFICER')`. Fixed with a standalone `npx prisma generate` — no dotenv wrapper and no `DATABASE_URL` (generate only reads the schema); `npm run db:generate` would NOT work here because that script is `dotenv -e ../../.env` and this workflow only writes `.env.test`. **Fault 2 — there was no passphrase.** The repository has **zero** Actions secrets, so `BACKUP_DRILL_ENCRYPTION_KEY` was never set and the drill step would have exited 1 even once the seed was fixed. Fixing only fault 1 would have moved the red one step later. Now generates an EPHEMERAL per-run passphrase, secret still overriding when present. **Fault 3, the real find — the verification compared ESTIMATES and called the drift data loss.** It summed `pg_stat_user_tables.n_live_tup` after an `ANALYZE`, with a comment asserting that made the comparison "exact, not approximate". `ANALYZE` SAMPLES; `n_live_tup` stays an estimate. Proved rather than assumed, on ONE database with no restore involved: estimate **4,142,466** vs true count **4,141,630** — an 836-row phantom. Replaced with an exact per-table `count(*)` via `query_to_xml`, placed AFTER `END_EPOCH` so verification time is never charged against the RTO target; verified there are no partitioned or inherited tables (0 and 0, 130 base tables) that could double-count. **Verification**: the drill now runs locally end to end — **PASS**, 4,141,630 original vs 4,141,630 restored, 638s against the 900s RTO target, exit 0. | **Fixing the first failure in a stack tells you nothing about the second.** Three weeks of red were three different bugs; the third only became visible once the first two were fixed and the drill could reach its own summary. Check what comes AFTER the failing step before calling it fixed. **A comment asserting a property is not the property** — "ANALYZE makes this exact" was wrong, and one query against a single database disproved it in seconds. **Do not weaken a check that might be catching something real**: the estimate-vs-exact measurement is what established the FAIL was the metric and not the restore, and the fixed drill then matched that exact number on both sides. **The repo has NO Actions secrets** — any workflow written to require one has never run. **An ephemeral passphrase is the right key for this drill**, not a missing long-lived one: it proves the round trip against a throwaway seeded database and keeps a real credential out of a job with no real data to protect. |

## Cloning this repo

```bash
git clone --recurse-submodules https://github.com/SHOUQALMAHARBAH/IBMS-APP.git
# already cloned without it?
git submodule update --init --recursive
```

The submodule pins a specific `ibms-brain` commit — it does not auto-track `main`. Pull
in newer brain rules deliberately:

```bash
cd ibms-brain && git pull origin main && cd ..
git add ibms-brain && git commit -m "ibms-brain: sync to latest"
```

## What's here today

Infrastructure (Part A + Part B), plus Part C **Domain A, Processes 1–10** — Lead
Management (#1), Prospect Management (#2), Customer Acquisition/Onboarding (#3-4, with
*simulated* screening), Needs Assessment (#5), Risk Assessment (#6), Product
Recommendation / Program Design (#7), Cross-Selling (#8, a no-op until the Policy module
lands), Up-Selling (#9, flags a survey that outgrew the designed property Sum Insured),
Relationship Management / CRM (#10, logs every touchpoint as an `Interaction` and serves
the aggregated 360° customer timeline — policies/claims/complaints empty until Domains
B/C/E land). **Domain A is complete.** Part C **Domain B** has begun with RFQ / Market
Submission (#11) — a minimal `Opportunity` parent (created from a FINALIZED Insurance
Program) plus `RFQ`/`RFQInsurer` (one RFQ per line, insurer shortlist, per-insurer
response tracking, a nightly business-day follow-up alert sweep). This paragraph predates
Domain B #12–22, Domains C–H and Parts D–F, all of which have since been built —
**root `README.md` § Scope status is the authority, not this summary.** Part F's
bilingual UI is complete: all 93 screens — the 89 under `app/(app)/` and the four
`(auth)` screens that render before sign-in — read from
`apps/web/lib/i18n/translations/`, including the 403 permission-denied branch
(see § What's New). Part G's final verification
checklist is complete — all seven items closed and guarded. See § Known gaps for
each built item's deferred edges.

**Custom office-scoped RBAC is IN PROGRESS — Phases 1, 2 and 3 of 5 have landed.** The
fixed 11-role catalogue is gone as an architecture: `Role` carries `organizationId`, a
role name is unique per office rather than globally, and authorization resolves
permissions from `roleId` — never from a role name, because two offices may each define
a "Manager" with different grants. `Permission` stays a single global catalogue; only
the grants are per-office. The legacy 11 survive as ordinary per-office rows carrying
their old machine names, so nobody's access changed. An office now DEFINES its own roles
at `/settings/roles`, every office has an `OFFICE_ADMINISTRATOR`, and a new office gets
that role and nothing else.

Before touching anything role-related read **`docs/office-scoped-rbac.md`** — it
carries these four constraints in full, the things that will bite, and the Phase 4
hand-off:

1. **A role NAME is not an identity.** `PermissionRepository.findCodesForRoles` and
   `PermissionsService` both take ids, deliberately — a name-keyed lookup returns every
   same-named role across every office and hands the caller their union. The same
   applies at the API boundary: role assignment is id-addressed
   (`{ roleId }` / `{ roleIds }`), and the by-name repository lookups were deleted, not
   left dormant.
2. **An RLS policy is only enforceable on a model carrying `organizationId`**, because
   that is how `tenantScopeExtension` decides whether to set `app.current_org_id` at
   all. A policy on any other table matches nothing, forever.
3. **`Role.status` has to reach every query that DECIDES access**, not just the screen.
   A retired role that still satisfied the authorization read or the
   last-administrator count would keep granting everything it ever granted.
4. **`isSystem` grants nothing.** It stops the Role screen renaming, retiring or
   re-granting a row and is read nowhere else. A flag called "system" on a Role is
   exactly where an `if (isSystem) allow` bypass gets smuggled in; there is a test
   whose only job is to prove it does not exist.

Phases 4 and 5 (the unified screen's org-structure half, subdomain resolution, the
business-function routing table that retires the two remaining role-name sites; then
migration rehearsal and dropping the `RoleName` enum type) are not built.

**Insurer CRUD HAS shipped** — API and screens, with `insurer.create`, `insurer.update`, `insurer.deactivate`,
`insurer.read`, `insurer.directory.read` and `insurer.office-form.map` granted. **CORRECTED 2026-10-19: this said `insurer.relationship.manage`, WHICH DOES NOT EXIST** — Phase 1 split that umbrella five ways because it gated two ENTITIES (an office's insurer records and the insurance LINES those records carry), and the sentence was never updated. Anybody handed this file would have gone looking for a code that is not in the graph. This
paragraph said it was "deliberately deferred" for the whole branch that built it, which
is the doc rot `meta/lex/definition-of-done.md` names: a deferral is true when written
and nobody revisits it. What survives of that deferral is the property it protected —
those codes sit OUTSIDE the office administrator's strict-subset-at-migration set and are
listed explicitly in `ADDED_AFTER_THE_MIGRATION`, so a fifth cannot slip in without a
decision.

**Before touching anything insurer-related read `docs/insurer-management.md`** — the
eleven constraints in full, including the two that are easiest to break by accident: the
canonical name key lives in the DATABASE and the TypeScript mirror is for suggestions
only, and an insurer row holds two kinds of fact where only the company-level half may
ever leave the office. Its two siblings go deeper on the global catalogue
(`docs/insurer-master-registry.md`) and the cross-office directory
(`docs/insurer-directory.md`).

## Common commands

```bash
npm install
cp .env.example .env
docker compose up -d db
npm run db:migrate:dev
npm run db:seed       # per-office roles + the global permission catalogue — RBAC needs these
npm run dev          # web:3000, api:4000
npm run lint
npm run typecheck
npm run test          # vitest, web + api

# api e2e — separate test DB (db-test), never the dev DB above. See README
# § Dev DB vs. test DB.
cp .env.test.example .env.test
docker compose up -d db-test
npm run db:test:migrate:dev
npm run test:e2e

npm run e2e           # playwright + axe-core, web

# Demo data for two Organizations, through the real HTTP API — DEV DB only,
# never .env.test. DEMO_PASSWORD is REQUIRED and has no default: the script
# refuses to start without one, because the password it used to carry was a
# literal in this repo. Sign in with it, then pair an authenticator at
# Settings -> Security. See apps/api/scripts/README-SEED-DEMO.md.
$env:DEMO_PASSWORD = (Read-Host "Choose a demo password")   # PowerShell
npm run seed:demo -w api

# Releases the demo logins from an MFA prompt nobody can answer — the repair
# for "I entered the correct code and it says the code is wrong" on an account
# the seed enrolled with a secret it never showed anyone. Passwords untouched.
npm run demo:release -w api

# The SAME repair for the sample.* accounts, which demo:release cannot reach:
# it matches the `demo.` prefix only, so it reports "No demo account is locked"
# and changes nothing. Four of eleven were locked this way on 2026-10-11.
npm run sample:release -w api
```

**Every screen is held to `docs/frontend-ux-directive.md`** — the owner's standing frontend and UX
instruction, in the repo because it reached one session as a message and the next would not have seen
it. Read it before building a screen. Its four-state requirement, its rule that a control a role
cannot use does not exist on their screen, and its reservation of implementation detail to the
System/Security Administrator are the three that change code most often. **It overlaps
`docs/b7-consistency-record.md` and disagrees with it in four places** — the header of the directive
states which, and those four are the owner's to settle, not to merge.

**Handing the system to someone who does not read code: `docs/first-run.md`** — the
bilingual (AR-first) first-sign-in walkthrough, walked against a running stack rather than
written from the source. It carries the measured per-account permission table, so if the
seed's role grants move, re-measure it rather than editing it by hand.

## Environment

- Node `20.19.0` — see `.nvmrc`. This satisfies Prisma 7's Node floor (≥20.19/22.12/24)
  but Prisma itself stays pinned at `6.19.3` in `package.json` — Node version no longer
  blocks a Prisma 7 install, so don't bump `prisma`/`@prisma/client` past 6.x without
  doing the driver-adapter (`@prisma/adapter-pg`) + `prisma.config.ts` migration first.
  See the Prisma note in root `README.md`.
- Docker required for Postgres locally and for building `apps/api`/`apps/web` images.

## Repo map

```
apps/web/     Next.js frontend
apps/api/     NestJS backend
packages/db/  Shared Prisma schema + client (@ibms/db)
ibms-brain/   Submodule — standards/rules/context (loaded via the import above)
.claude/      Agents/commands mirrored from ibms-brain + a subset of its hooks — see
              README.md § .claude/ — agents, commands, hooks
```

`@code-reviewer` and `@software-developer` (defined in `ibms-brain/meta/agents/`) and
`/brain-gap` are available in this repo via `.claude/`. Not yet wired here: the `git push`
evidence-gate hook (`enforce-evidence.sh`) and the domain-code hooks — see README.md for why.

## Before you write code

Same rule as ibms-brain: read `meta/context/` for the area, `meta/lex/` for what's
mandatory, cite the source document (PDPL / CBJ / ISO 27001/27701 / a specific
`PRIV-STD-*`/`PRIV-SOP-*`) in the PR when the change touches a regulatory obligation.
